The analyzed variables included socioeconomic profile, flock characteristics, sanitary practices, knowledge about migratory birds and avian influenza, and interest in educational strategies.
Core methods
Data were analyzed using descriptive statistics and exploratory association analyses.
Main results
A predominance of keepers with low formal education was observed, along with multispecies flocks mainly intended for self-consumption and limited adoption of basic biosecurity measures.

Limitations and evidence strength
The supplied evidence does not report specific limitations or evidence strength assessment.

AFB1 通过损害保护性分节丝状细菌的肠道定植来促进 H3N8 在鸡中的复制

AFB1 promotes H3N8 replication in chickens by impairing gut colonization of protective segmented filamentous bacteria
作者： Zhenkai Dai, Mengyue Dong, Hainuo Zeng, Yuhang Bai, Runlin Shao, Ruiheng Liu, Qian Wang, Jiaqian Rao, Tong Li, Yuanjia Liu
摘要中文翻译Abstract AFB1,a common feed mycotoxin,can worsen viral infections.虽然 H3N8 禽流感 主要影响鸟类并可能损害肠道健康,但 AFB1 和 H3N8 感染对家禽肠道的综合影响仍不清楚。We show that environmentally relevant AFB1 levels (25–100 μg/kg) significantly enhance H3N8 replication in vitro and in vivo.综合组织病理学、微生物组和代谢组学分析显示,AFB1 诱导的肠道屏障功能障碍和有益细菌(真杆菌、Subdoligrinum)的特异性消耗,与十二烷二酸的减少相关。Critically,AFB1 impaired ileal colonization of segmented filamentous bacteria (SFB),creating a permissive environment for viral propagation. Intervention with SFB supplementation reversed AFB1-mediated enhancement of H3N8 infection.从机制上讲,AFB1 通过损害粘膜完整性和 SFB 定植来破坏肠道稳态,从而放大 H3N8 发病机制。这些发现建立了一种通过肠道菌群失调介导的新型霉菌毒素-病毒相互作用轴,强调在家禽生产中迫切需要 AFB1 控制策略以减轻流感风险。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在调查 AFB1(一种常见的饲料霉菌毒素)如何影响病毒感染,特别是其可能加剧 H3N8 禽流感 对家禽肠道健康的影响。
设计与对象
该研究使用环境相关的 AFB1 水平(25-100 μg/kg)在体外和体内进行,重点关注鸡中的 H3N8 禽流感。
核心方法
研究人员利用综合组织病理学、微生物组和代谢组学分析来评估 AFB1 对 H3N8 感染鸡的肠道屏障功能和细菌定植的影响。
主要结果
分析表明,AFB1 会诱导肠道屏障功能障碍,并特异性消耗真杆菌和 Subdolicapsulum 等有益细菌,并降低十二烷二酸水平。
解释与创新
研究结果揭示了 AFB1 通过破坏肠道稳态和 SFB 定植来增强 H3N8 复制的新机制,强调了霉菌毒素和 禽流感 之间以前未探索过的相互作用。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果表明,控制家禽饲料中的 AFB1 水平可能是降低 禽流感 暴发 风险和改善家禽肠道健康的关键策略。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于体外和体内模型,证据基于抽象水平的报告,可能无法完全捕捉现场的复杂性。证据强度中等。

AFB1 promotes H3N8 replication in chickens by impairing gut colonization of protective segmented filamentous bacteria

Authors: Zhenkai Dai, Mengyue Dong, Hainuo Zeng, Yuhang Bai, Runlin Shao, Ruiheng Liu, Qian Wang, Jiaqian Rao, Tong Li, Yuanjia Liu
Original abstract (English)
Abstract AFB1, a common feed mycotoxin, can worsen viral infections. While H3N8 avian influenza mainly affects birds and can harm gut health, the combined impact of AFB1 and H3N8 infection on poultry intestines remains unknown. We show that environmentally relevant AFB1 levels (25–100 μg/kg) significantly enhance H3N8 replication in vitro and in vivo. Integrated histopathological, microbiome, and metabolomic analyses revealed AFB1-induced gut barrier dysfunction and specific depletion of beneficial bacteria (Eubacterium, Subdoligranulum), correlating with reduced dodecanedioic acid. Critically, AFB1 impaired ileal colonization of segmented filamentous bacteria (SFB), creating a permissive environment for viral propagation. Intervention with SFB supplementation reversed AFB1-mediated enhancement of H3N8 infection. Mechanistically, AFB1 disrupts intestinal homeostasis by compromising mucosal integrity and SFB colonization, thereby amplifying H3N8 pathogenesis. These findings establish a novel mycotoxin-virus interaction axis mediated through gut dysbiosis, underscoring the urgent need for AFB1 control strategies in poultry production to mitigate influenza risks.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to investigate how AFB1, a common feed mycotoxin, affects viral infections, particularly its potential to worsen the impact of H3N8 avian influenza on poultry gut health.
Design and population
The study was conducted in vitro and in vivo using environmentally relevant AFB1 levels (25–100 μg/kg) and focused on H3N8 avian influenza in chickens.
Core methods
The researchers used integrated histopathological, microbiome, and metabolomic analyses to assess the effects of AFB1 on gut barrier function and bacterial colonization in H3N8-infected chickens.
Main results
The analyses showed that AFB1 induced gut barrier dysfunction and specific depletion of beneficial bacteria such as Eubacterium and Subdoligranulum, with a reduction in dodecanedioic acid levels.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings reveal a novel mechanism by which AFB1 enhances H3N8 replication through disruption of intestinal homeostasis and SFB colonization, highlighting a previously unexplored interaction between mycotoxins and avian influenza.
Scientific and public-health significance
These results suggest that controlling AFB1 levels in poultry feed could be a critical strategy to reduce the risk of avian influenza outbreaks and improve gut health in birds.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to in vitro and in vivo models, and the evidence is based on abstract-level reporting, which may not fully capture in-field complexities. The evidence strength is moderate.

A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

破译病毒对 宿主 基因组 结构的作用:计算物理视角。

Deciphering viral action on host genome structure: a computational physics perspective.
作者： Andrea Fontana, Andrea M. Chiariello
摘要中文翻译最近的实验结果表明,几种病毒能够基本上重新组织 宿主 细胞 基因组 架构。由于染色质三维​​ (3D) 组织与重要的细胞功能紧密相关,因此研究病毒感染如何影响塑造 DNA 折叠的物理机制,为了解病毒对基因调控和随后激活的作用提供了有用的线索。本综述概述了计算建模方法的最新进展,这些方法用于阐明 宿主 细胞染色质 3D 结构在病毒感染(包括 SARS-CoV-2 和 禽流感 IAV-H5N1)后如何改变。鉴于这种方法的灵活性,此类模型可以适应多种病原体,使其成为研究不同感染机制特异性的有价值的方法。总的来说,我们展示了这些模型如何成为有用的工具,与传统的实验方法相结合,可以从新的角度破译分子水平上感染机制的复杂性。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述重点关注 禽流感 A 病毒,特别研究这些病毒如何通过计算建模方法改变 宿主 细胞 基因组 结构。核心问题是病毒感染如何影响形成 DNA 折叠的物理机制,从而为基因调控和激活提供见解。
证据基础与方法
该综述对用于研究 禽流感 A 病毒对 宿主 染色质 3D 结构影响的计算建模方法进行了系统分析。它包括对 SARS-CoV-2 和 禽流感 IAV-H5N1 的研究,强调了这些模型在适应各种病原体方面的灵活性。
共识与结论
该综述的结论是,计算模型是理解 禽流感 A 病毒如何重组 宿主 基因组 的有效工具。这些模型与传统的实验方法相结合,为破译感染机制的分子复杂性提供了新的视角。
争议与缺口
该评论承认,虽然计算模型提供了有价值的见解,但它们可能无法完全捕捉病毒与 宿主 DNA 相互作用的细微差别。需要对 禽流感 亚型的具体机制进行更详细的研究,特别是在不同的 宿主 人群中。
科研与实践启示
该综述表明,进一步的研究应集中于完善计算模型,以更好地反映 禽流感 亚型的生物学特异性。它还强调了将这些模型与实验数据相结合的重要性,以增进理解并为公共卫生策略提供信息。

Deciphering viral action on host genome structure: a computational physics perspective.

Authors: Andrea Fontana, Andrea M. Chiariello
Original abstract (English)
Recent experimental findings indicate that several viruses are able to substantially re-organize the host cell genome architecture. Since chromatin three-dimensional (3D) organization is tightly linked to vital cellular functions, investigating how viral infections impact physical mechanisms shaping DNA folding provides useful clues about virus action on gene regulation and consequent activation. This review provides an overview of recent advances in the development of computational modelling approaches used to shed light on how host cell chromatin 3D structure is altered following viral infections, including SARS-CoV-2 and avian influenza IAV-H5N1. Given the flexibility of this approach, such models can be adapted to a wide range of pathogens, making them valuable methods for investigating the specificity of distinct infection mechanisms. Overall, we show how such models can be helpful tools that, in tandem with traditional experimental methods, allow to decipher complexity of infection mechanisms at molecular level from a new perspective.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on avian influenza A viruses, specifically examining how these viruses alter the host cell genome structure through computational modeling approaches. The central question is how viral infections impact the physical mechanisms that shape DNA folding, offering insights into gene regulation and activation.
Evidence base and method
The review is a systematic analysis of computational modeling approaches used to study the effects of avian influenza A viruses on host chromatin 3D structure. It includes studies on SARS-CoV-2 and avian influenza IAV-H5N1, highlighting the flexibility of these models in adapting to various pathogens.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that computational models are effective tools for understanding how avian influenza A viruses reorganize the host genome. These models, when combined with traditional experimental methods, provide a new perspective on deciphering the molecular complexity of infection mechanisms.
Controversies and gaps
The review acknowledges that while computational models offer valuable insights, they may not fully capture the nuances of viral interactions with host DNA. There is a need for more detailed studies on the specific mechanisms of avian influenza subtypes, particularly in diverse host populations.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that further research should focus on refining computational models to better reflect the biological specificity of avian influenza subtypes. It also emphasizes the importance of integrating these models with experimental data to improve understanding and inform public health strategies.

重新审视游轮上汉坦病毒和其他人畜共患病毒的动物 储存宿主:影响和预防

Revisiting Animal Reservoirs of Hantavirus and Other Zoonotic Viruses on Cruise Ships: Impact and Prevention
作者： D. Katterine Bonilla-Aldana, Jorge L. Bonilla-Aldana, Ivan Camilo Sanchez-Rojas, Lysien I Zambrano, Alfonso J. Rodriguez-Morales
摘要中文翻译国际海上旅游业的持续增长和邮轮业的扩张增加了新出现的人畜共患病的重要性,成为对全球公共卫生的重大挑战。游轮构成了复杂的流行病学环境,其特点是人口密度高、国际流动性快、人类、动物和沿海环境之间不断相互作用,这些因素有利于新出现的传染源的引入和传播。主要威胁包括汉坦病毒和其他人畜共患病毒,例如登革热、基孔肯雅热、寨卡病毒、黄热病和 禽流感,这些病毒与港口、旅游目的地和热带生态系统中存在的动物 储存宿主 和 媒介 相关。本综述研究了汉坦病毒和与海上旅游相关的其他人畜共患病原体的动物生态学,包括城郊啮齿动物、蝙蝠、候鸟和其他哺乳动物。它讨论了气候变化、沿海城市化和环境变化对病毒 传播 动态的影响。强调了与国际邮轮相关的流行病学风险,特别是生态旅游游览期间的暴露、病原体的引入以及海上 监测 的挑战。该审查还涉及旅行医学、生物安全和感染控制,强调“同一个健康”方法。加强综合 监测 和预防系统对于确保 21 世纪的海上​​旅游业更安全、更可持续和更有弹性至关重要。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述研究了海上旅游环境中与动物 储存宿主 相关的人畜共患病毒,重点关注汉坦病毒和其他病原体,如登革热、基孔肯雅热、寨卡病毒、黄热病和 禽流感,在游轮作为复杂的流行病学环境的背景下。核心问题涉及这些病毒对人类、动物和沿海环境相互作用的高密度、国际流动环境中的公共卫生的影响。
证据基础与方法
该综述在范围上是叙述性的,不报告系统的综述方法、数据库、检索日期、资格标准或研究选择过程。它综合了有关动物 储存宿主(例如城郊啮齿动物、蝙蝠、候鸟)生态学的一般知识,并讨论了影响病毒 传播 动态的气候变化和沿海城市化等因素。
共识与结论
该审查强调,由于人口密度高、国际流动性快和环境相互作用,游轮是人畜共患病毒引入和传播的高风险场所。它将汉坦病毒和其他人畜共患病原体(例如登革热、基孔肯雅热、寨卡病毒、黄热病、禽流感)确定为与港口和旅游目的地动物 储存宿主 相关的主要威胁。
争议与缺口
该审查没有提供与 禽流感 或游轮环境中其他人畜共患病毒相关的争议、相互矛盾的发现或未解决的机制的具体证据。除了有关海事 监测 挑战的一般性陈述外,它缺乏详细的方法论批评或 监测 系统中的差距。
科研与实践启示
该审查强调需要加强综合 监测 和预防系统,以减轻海上旅游中的流行病学风险,特别是在生态旅游游览期间。它倡导“同一个健康”方法并改进生物安全和感染控制措施,以确保更安全的海上旅游。

Revisiting Animal Reservoirs of Hantavirus and Other Zoonotic Viruses on Cruise Ships: Impact and Prevention

Authors: D. Katterine Bonilla-Aldana, Jorge L. Bonilla-Aldana, Ivan Camilo Sanchez-Rojas, Lysien I Zambrano, Alfonso J. Rodriguez-Morales
Original abstract (English)
The sustained growth of international maritime tourism and the expansion of the cruise industry have increased the importance of emerging zoonotic diseases as a significant challenge to global public health. Cruise ships constitute complex epidemiological settings characterized by high population density, rapid international mobility, and constant interaction between humans, animals, and coastal environments, factors that favor the introduction and spread of emerging infectious agents. Among the main threats are hantaviruses and other zoonotic viruses, such as dengue, chikungunya, Zika, yellow fever, and avian influenza, which are associated with animal reservoirs and vectors present in ports, tourist destinations, and tropical ecosystems. This review examines the ecology of animal reservoirs of hantaviruses and other zoonotic pathogens relevant to maritime tourism, including peri-urban rodents, bats, migratory birds, and other mammals. It discusses the impact of climate change, coastal urbanization, and environmental change on viral transmission dynamics. Epidemiological risks linked to international cruises are highlighted, particularly exposure during ecotourism excursions, pathogen introduction, and challenges for maritime surveillance. The review also addresses travel medicine, biosecurity, and infection control, emphasizing a One Health approach. Strengthening integrated surveillance and prevention systems is essential to ensure safer, more sustainable, and resilient maritime tourism in the 21st century.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines zoonotic viruses associated with animal reservoirs in maritime tourism settings, focusing on hantaviruses and other pathogens such as dengue, chikungunya, Zika, yellow fever, and avian influenza, within the context of cruise ships as complex epidemiological environments. The central question addresses the impact of these viruses on public health in high-density, internationally mobile settings where humans, animals, and coastal environments interact.
Evidence base and method
The review is narrative in scope and does not report systematic review methods, databases, search dates, eligibility criteria, or study selection processes. It synthesizes general knowledge about the ecology of animal reservoirs (e.g., peri-urban rodents, bats, migratory birds) and discusses factors such as climate change and coastal urbanization affecting viral transmission dynamics.
Consensus and conclusions
The review highlights cruise ships as high-risk settings for the introduction and spread of zoonotic viruses due to high population density, rapid international mobility, and environmental interactions. It identifies hantaviruses and other zoonotic pathogens (e.g., dengue, chikungunya, Zika, yellow fever, avian influenza) as key threats linked to animal reservoirs in ports and tourist destinations.
Controversies and gaps
The review does not provide specific evidence on controversies, conflicting findings, or unresolved mechanisms related to avian influenza or other zoonotic viruses in cruise ship settings. It lacks detailed methodological critiques or gaps in surveillance systems beyond general statements about challenges in maritime surveillance.
Research and practice implications
The review emphasizes the need for strengthening integrated surveillance and prevention systems to mitigate epidemiological risks in maritime tourism, particularly during ecotourism excursions. It advocates for a One Health approach and improved biosecurity and infection control measures to ensure safer maritime tourism.

欧洲后院家禽的病原体 患病率 和健康风险:范围界定审查

Pathogen prevalence and health risks in European backyard poultry: a scoping review
作者： Clément Ngandjui Yonga, Sandrina Vandenput, Jean‐Luc Hornick, Didier Marlier
摘要中文翻译由于对当地生产的鸡蛋和肉类的兴趣日益浓厚,后院家禽饲养在欧洲范围内不断扩大。然而,这些小型且通常未登记的鸡群可能在有限的兽医监督和可变的生物安全下运作,为家禽、商业家禽、野生动物和人类之间的接触创造了机会。尽管存在这些担忧,欧洲后院家禽系统中病原体出现的证据仍然分散且异质。本次范围审查旨在识别和综合欧洲后院家禽群中报告的病原体的现有证据。具体来说,它试图:(i)绘制报告的寄生虫、细菌和病毒病原体的发生情况;(ii) 描述所使用的诊断方法;(iii) 总结纳入研究中报告的与病原体检测或 传播 相关的因素;(iv) 强调未来 监测 和研究的方法论差距和优先事项。审查是根据 PRISMA-ScR 指南进行的。截至 2024 年 7 月,检索均在 PubMed、CAB Abstracts 和 Scopus 中进行。在欧洲进行的针对后院或小规模家禽群的研究,如果报告了病原体的发生、诊断方法或相关因素,则符合资格。来自 17 个欧洲国家的 33 项研究符合纳入标准。提取的数据包括研究设计、鸡群特征、调查的病原体、诊断方法、报告的发生率或 患病率 值以及相关因素。本综述表明,欧洲后院家禽群中报告了多种寄生虫、细菌和病毒病原体,包括艾美耳球虫属、鸡皮癣菌、鸡毒支原体、沙门氏菌属、禽流感 病毒、传染性支气管炎病毒和马立克氏病病毒。一些研究报告了病原体检测频率较高,并描述了几个相关因素,包括户外接触、多物种饲养、与 野生鸟类 接触以及有限的生物安全。然而,由于地理覆盖范围不均匀、主要是横截面研究设计、频繁使用便利抽样、异构诊断方法以及抽样单位的差异,证据基础仍然支离破碎。因此,研究结果应被解释为报告的病原体发生和相关因素的映射,而不是对真正的欧洲 患病率 的估计或 储存宿主 状态的证据。需要改进 监测、统一的诊断方案和纵向研究,以更好地描述病原体循环及其在“同一个健康”框架内对动物和公共卫生的影响。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述在“同一个健康”框架内检查了欧洲后院家禽群中病原体的发生情况,重点关注寄生虫、细菌和病毒病原体,包括 禽流感 病毒。中心问题涉及绘制这些系统中报告的事件、诊断方法、相关因素和方法差距。
证据基础与方法
本次范围界定审查遵循 PRISMA-ScR 指南,并检索了截至 2024 年 7 月的 PubMed、CAB Abstracts 和 Scopus。符合条件的研究在欧洲进行,重点关注后院或小规模家禽群,报告病原体的发生、诊断方法或相关因素。来自 17 个欧洲国家的 33 项研究符合纳入标准。
共识与结论
据报道,欧洲后院家禽群中存在多种病原体,包括 禽流感 病毒以及艾美耳球虫属、鸡皮眼病菌、鸡毒支原体、沙门氏菌属、传染性支气管炎病毒和马立克氏病病毒。描述了几个相关因素,例如户外访问、多物种饲养、与 野生鸟类 的接触以及有限的生物安全。
争议与缺口
The evidence base is fragmented due to uneven geographical coverage,predominantly cross-sectional designs,convenience sampling,heterogeneous diagnostic methods,and differences in sampling units.结果应被解释为报告事件的映射,而不是真实的 患病率 估计或 储存宿主 状态的证据。
科研与实践启示
需要改进 监测、统一的诊断方案和纵向研究,以更好地描述病原体循环及其在“同一个健康”框架内对动物和公共卫生的影响。

Pathogen prevalence and health risks in European backyard poultry: a scoping review

Authors: Clément Ngandjui Yonga, Sandrina Vandenput, Jean‐Luc Hornick, Didier Marlier
Original abstract (English)
Backyard poultry keeping has expanded across Europe, driven by increasing interest in locally produced eggs and meat. However, these small and often unregistered flocks may operate with limited veterinary supervision and variable biosecurity, creating opportunities for contact between domestic birds, commercial poultry, wildlife, and humans. Despite these concerns, evidence on pathogen occurrence in European backyard poultry systems remains scattered and heterogeneous. This scoping review aimed to identify and synthesize the available evidence on pathogens reported in European backyard poultry flocks. Specifically, it sought to: (i) map reported occurrences of parasitic, bacterial, and viral pathogens; (ii) describe the diagnostic methods used; (iii) summarize factors associated with pathogen detection or transmission as reported in the included studies; and (iv) highlight methodological gaps and priorities for future surveillance and research. The review was conducted according to PRISMA-ScR guidelines. Searches were performed in PubMed, CAB Abstracts, and Scopus up to July 2024. Studies conducted in Europe and focusing on backyard or small-scale poultry flocks were eligible if they reported pathogen occurrence, diagnostic methods, or associated factors. Thirty-three studies from 17 European countries met the inclusion criteria. Data extracted included study design, flock characteristics, pathogens investigated, diagnostic approaches, reported occurrence or prevalence values, and associated factors. This review shows that a wide range of parasitic, bacterial, and viral pathogens has been reported in European backyard poultry flocks, including Eimeria spp., Dermanyssus gallinae , Mycoplasma gallisepticum , Salmonella spp., avian influenza virus, infectious bronchitis virus, and Marek’s disease virus. Some studies reported high pathogen detection frequencies, and several associated factors were described, including outdoor access, multispecies keeping, contact with wild birds, and limited biosecurity. However, the evidence base remains fragmented due to uneven geographical coverage, predominantly cross-sectional study designs, frequent use of convenience sampling, heterogeneous diagnostic methods, and differences in sampling units. Therefore, the findings should be interpreted as a mapping of reported pathogen occurrence and associated factors, rather than as estimates of true European prevalence or evidence of reservoir status. Improved surveillance, harmonized diagnostic protocols, and longitudinal studies are needed to better characterize pathogen circulation and its implications for animal and public health within a One Health framework.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines pathogen occurrence in European backyard poultry flocks, focusing on parasitic, bacterial, and viral pathogens, including avian influenza virus, within a One Health framework. The central question addresses mapping reported occurrences, diagnostic methods, associated factors, and methodological gaps in these systems.
Evidence base and method
This scoping review followed PRISMA-ScR guidelines and searched PubMed, CAB Abstracts, and Scopus up to July 2024. Eligible studies were conducted in Europe and focused on backyard or small-scale poultry flocks, reporting pathogen occurrence, diagnostic methods, or associated factors. Thirty-three studies from 17 European countries met inclusion criteria.
Consensus and conclusions
A wide range of pathogens has been reported in European backyard poultry flocks, including avian influenza virus alongside Eimeria spp., Dermanyssus gallinae, Mycoplasma gallisepticum, Salmonella spp., infectious bronchitis virus, and Marek’s disease virus. Several associated factors were described, such as outdoor access, multispecies keeping, contact with wild birds, and limited biosecurity.
Controversies and gaps
The evidence base is fragmented due to uneven geographical coverage, predominantly cross-sectional designs, convenience sampling, heterogeneous diagnostic methods, and differences in sampling units. Findings should be interpreted as a mapping of reported occurrences rather than true prevalence estimates or evidence of reservoir status.
Research and practice implications
Improved surveillance, harmonized diagnostic protocols, and longitudinal studies are needed to better characterize pathogen circulation and its implications for animal and public health within a One Health framework.

H5N1 禽流感(H5 禽流感) - 新南威尔士州第一产业部

H5N1 Avian influenza (H5 bird flu) - NSW Department of Primary Industries
新闻简报新南威尔士州第一产业部证实,澳大利亚南部海岸的几种流浪迁徙海鸟体内存在 H5N1 禽流感(H5 禽流感)。这是一个重大的生物安全事件,因为 H5N1 是 禽流感 的高致病性菌株。该部门正在通过 BioResponse NSW 应用程序实时更新来积极管理局势,并鼓励公众报告任何害虫或疾病。虽然没有提供具体病例或死亡人数,但确认强调了在监测 禽流感 暴发 时保持警惕的必要性。该部门还通过专门的热线和资源解决其他生物安全问题,例如牛蜱和蜱热。本报告强调了新南威尔士州为保障动物健康和福利所做的持续努力。

A high priorityPublic-health news

H5N1 Avian influenza (H5 bird flu) - NSW Department of Primary Industries

News Brief
The NSW Department of Primary Industries confirmed the presence of H5N1 avian influenza (H5 bird flu) in several vagrant migratory seabirds on Australia's southern coast. This is a significant biosecurity event, as H5N1 is a high pathogenicity strain of avian influenza. The department is actively managing the situation through real-time updates via the BioResponse NSW app and encouraging the public to report any pests or diseases. While no specific case or death counts are provided, the confirmation highlights the need for vigilance in monitoring avian influenza outbreaks. The department is also addressing other biosecurity issues, such as cattle tick and tick fever, through dedicated hotlines and resources. This report underscores the ongoing efforts to safeguard animal health and welfare in NSW.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

中国新增 4 例人类 H9N2 禽流感病例,澳大利亚 野生鸟类 发现更多 H5N1 禽流感病例 - CIDRAP

4 new human H9N2 avian flu cases in China as Australia sees more H5N1 in wild birds - CIDRAP
新闻简报On 4 August 2026, Hong Kong’s Centre for Health Protection announced four new confirmed human cases of H9N2 avian influenza in mainland China. The patients—aged 3 to 72 years—developed symptoms between 5 and 13 July in Gansu, Jiangsu, Jiangxi, and Yunnan provinces. The announcement follows a six-month period in which 20 H9N2 infections were reported worldwide, including 12 on the mainland, one each in Hong Kong and Taiwan, and one in Italy. Since 2015, 164 human H9N2 cases, including two fatalities, have been reported to the World Health Organization in the Western Pacific. Authorities did not disclose clinical outcomes or public-health actions in the evidence provided.
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 8 月 4 日发布;活动日期为 2026 年 7 月 5 日至 13 日。
地点与对象
中国大陆(甘肃、江苏、江西、云南省)。
事件
香港卫生防护中心 (CHP) 报告新增四例人类感染 H9N2 禽流感 病例。
规模、影响与风险
新增四例;过去六个月,全球报告了 20 起已知的 H9N2 感染病例;自 2015 年以来,向世卫组织西太平洋地区报告的人类 H9N2 病例总数为 164 例(包括 2 例死亡)。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生防护中心没有提供有关病人预后或病程的资料;所提供的证据中没有报告其他公共卫生措施或风险评估。

Topics & Projects Antimicrobial Stewardship Chronic Wasting Disease Osterholm Update Podcasts Newsletters About CIDRAP CIDRAP in the News Our Staff Our Director Osterholm in the Press Shop Merchandise Contact Us About Search Support Menu 4 new human H9N2 avian flu cases in China as Australia sees more H5N1 in wild birds News brief August 4, 2026 Stephanie Soucheray, MA Topics Avian Influenza (Bird Flu) Share Juperiterimages / iStock Hong Kong's Centre for Health Protection (CHP) is reporting four new cases of H9N2 avian flu on mainland China. The case-patients include a 3-year-old girl from Gansu province, a 12-year-old boy from Jiangsu province, a 72-year-old woman from Jiangxi province, and a 58-year-old woman from Yunnan province. All patients had symptom onset between July 5 and July 13. CHP provided no additional information on the patients’ prognosis or course of illness. There have been 20 known human infections of H9N2 over the past six months, including 12 on mainland China, one each in Hong Kong and Taiwan, and one case in Italy. Since 2015, a total of 164 cases of human infection with H9N2 avian flu, including two deaths (both in people with underlying conditions), have been reported to the World Health Organization in the Western Pacific, the organization said. Australia now has 102 wild bird detections of H5N1 In other avian flu news, Australia is reporting scores of new wild bird detections of H5N1 in the weeks following the virus’ introduction to the continent. Between July 20 and today, detections rose from 20 to 102, with the majority of detections in South Australia. So far Australia has not detected the virus in poultry or the wider agriculture industry. Review: Self-collected HPV tests accurately detect pre-cancerous cells in women News brief August 4, 2026 Meghan Holohan Topics Human Papillomavirus (HPV) anastas / iStock A meta-analysis of 11 studies concludes that self-testing for human papilloma virus (HPV) using urine or vaginal samples accurately detects pre-cancerous cells caused by HPV, also known as high-grade squamous intraepithelial lesions (HSIL+). This paper, published in the Journal of Obstetrics and Gynaecology Research, reinforces the precision of self-collection testing for HPV, which could increase access to screening. “The outcomes of this investigation indicate that HPV testing performed using both self-collected urine specimen and vaginal specimens achieved a high sensitivity in detecting HSIL+,” wrote the Taiwanese authors. High sensitivity for self-collected vaginal swabs The World Health Organization (WHO) hopes to eliminate cervical cancer by 2030 and sees HPV self-collecting kits as one way to expand screening to reach more women, especially those who have not yet undergone HPV testing. The researchers hoped to understand the accuracy of self-testing methods by looking at studies that examined the precision of self-collected HPV kits in finding abnormal cells using urine or vaginal samples. The papers included in the analysis were based on 3,534 self-collected urine samples and 3,523 vaginal samples. The distribution of self-sampling kits has the potential to yield a low rate of adverse events and a high level of satisfaction among patients. Self-collection of urine samples had a pooled sensitivity of 81%, while self-testing with vaginal swabs had a pooled sensitivity of 88.6% for detecting pre-cancerous cells. “The meta-analysis corroborated the high sensitivity of HPV testing with self-collected vaginal specimens,” the authors wrote. “The distribution of self-sampling kits has the potential to yield a low rate of adverse events and a high level of satisfaction among patients.” Climate change is shifting malaria activity in Africa News brief August 4, 2026 Sarah Boden Topics Malaria Climate change is moving where the malaria hotbeds are in Africa, forcing new regions to contend with the mosquito-borne disease. The female Anopheles stephensi mosquito transmits malaria parasites. Credit: James Gathany / CDC Both the malaria parasite and the life cycle of the mosquito vectors, which are cold-blooded, are highly responsive to temperatures, making the disease sensitive to climate change. A recent study in Nature finds that rising temperatures have likely increased malaria in high-elevation East and southern Africa, where the weather is typically cooler. At the same time, climate change likely averted a comparable number of cases in West Africa, where temperatures are increasing to a point that it's hot enough to reduce transmission. The analysis used 50,425 surveys of malaria infection rates in sub-Saharan Africa, spanning 1900–2016, to calculate monthly averages of the disease at the state or province level. Then, researchers modeled how temperature and extreme precipitation shaped the prevalence of malaria in each region, controlling for factors like seasonality and malaria intervention efforts. "Over the coming century, we project that climate change could marginally accelerate the elimination of malaria in West and central Africa, where the present-day burden is highest," write the study authors, which include researchers from Yale University, the University of California, Berkeley, and the University of Cape Town. Malaria mitigation efforts have a larger impact than climate change Although the effects of climate change on malaria are meaningful, the authors caution that the impact is far smaller than the reductions that have been achieved through medical care, mosquito nets, vector control, and economic development. These investments, they argue, can counterbalance the effects of climate change in most places. "Malaria elimination within the next generation remains plausible, even in the face of climate change," they said. They add that future work should explore how emerging methods for predicting climate change could give public health agencies valuable information in preparing for and responding to malaria activity. More than one-third of invasive E coli isolates are multidrug-resistant, global analysis finds News brief August 4, 2026 Chris Dall, MA Topics Antimicrobial Stewardship E coli An analysis of invasive Escherichia coli isolates collected from 26 countries found that more than one-third were multidrug-resistant (MDR), researchers reported today in Clinical Infectious Diseases. CDC / Alissa Eckert The study, by researchers with drugmaker Johnson & Johnson, analyzed more than 10,000 E coli isolates collected from patients with bloodstream infections from 2011 through 2023. The aim of the study was to evaluate the antibiotic resistance, sequence type (ST), and O-serotype distribution of extraintestinal pathogenic E coli (ExPEC), a pathogen that can infect sites outside the intestines and cause invasive E coli disease, or IED, which includes life-threatening conditions like sepsis. “Increasing antimicrobial resistance rates and the emergence of multidrug-resistant (MDR) E. coli strains represent a major challenge in the treatment of IED,” the study authors wrote. “In particular, resistance to carbapenems, third-generation cephalosporins, aminoglycosides, or fluoroquinolones is an obstacle to successful treatment.” 34% of E coli isolates labeled as MDR Of the 10,098 ExPEC isolates collected, 34% were labeled as MDR, but there was a wide variation between countries. India had the highest MDR rate (83.3%), followed by Mexico (76.6%) and China (69.4%), while the Netherlands (12.1%), New Zealand (11.6%), and Sweden (10.6%) had the lowest MDR rates. Countries with lower income levels tended to have higher rates of MDR E coli. Overall, serotypes O25, O2, O6, and O1 were the most prevalent. Among the MDR isolates, O25 was the most prevalent in all countries (ranging from 11.3% in China to 56.2% in Mexico). ST131, a globally dominant clone and important cause of urinary tract and bloodstream infections worldwide, accounted for 33% of all MDR isolates. “Our findings stress the importance of epidemiological studies to monitor the prevalence of high-risk ExPEC clones and highlight the need for ongoing research into novel preventive and therapeutic strategies, including vaccines, to reduce the burden of bacterial disease and limit the spread of antimicrobial resistance,” the authors concluded. 澳大利亚警告野生动物传播范围扩大,H5N1禽流感导致数十只海鸟死亡 - AnewZ

H5N1 bird flu kills dozens of seabirds as Australia warns of wider wildlife spread - AnewZ
新闻简报澳大利亚确认首次发生海鸟大规模 H5N1 禽流感 死亡率 事件,在南澳大利亚雅法角附近海岸发现 50 只大凤头燕鸥死亡,并发现 35 只病鸟。农业部长朱莉·柯林斯 (Julie Collins) 表示,测试证实这些鸟类体内存在 H5N1 病毒,并警告可能会出现更广泛的传播和对野生动物的更大影响。虽然农场尚未检测到该病毒,但已经加强了生物安全措施以保护家禽。人类风险仍然很低,因为没有证据表明存在人与人之间的 传播。政府一直在通过加强生物安全、滨鸟测试和应对计划来应对该病毒。该事件于 2026 年 8 月 3 日报道,大规模 死亡率 发生于 2026 年 7 月 31 日。部长强调,由于病毒通过野生动物和自然环境传播,可能会导致重大损失,因此需要保持警惕。目前情况正在调查中,预计会有更多细节。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该事件于 2026 年 8 月 3 日报告,大规模 死亡率 事件发生于 2026 年 7 月 31 日星期五,警告于 2026 年 8 月 3 日星期一发出。
地点与对象
该事件发生在南澳大利亚海岸附近,特别是贾法角附近,距离阿德莱德约 250 公里。它涉及海鸟,特别是大凤头燕鸥,并影响了更广泛的澳大利亚人口。
事件
澳大利亚确认首例海鸟大规模 H5N1 禽流感 死亡率 事件,造成 50 只大凤头燕鸥死亡,并发现 35 只患病燕鸥。农业部长 Julie Collins 表示,测试证实这些鸟类中存在 H5N1。
规模、影响与风险
H5N1 禽流感已导致大量野生动物死亡,50 只海鸟死亡,35 只患病。这位部长警告说,受影响的野生动物可能会更广泛、数量更多。人类风险仍然很低,因为该病毒尚未在农场发现,也不会在人与人之间传播。
应对、状态与不确定性
澳大利亚已在农场实施强化生物安全措施,并正在进行 监测 和测试。部长强调了准备和应对计划,但传播和影响的全部范围仍在调查中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

H5N1 bird flu kills dozens of seabirds as Australia warns of wider wildlife spread - AnewZ

News Brief
Australia has confirmed its first mass mortality event of H5N1 bird flu among seabirds, with 50 greater crested terns killed and 35 sick birds found off the coast of South Australia near Cape Jaffa. Agriculture Minister Julie Collins stated that testing confirmed the presence of H5N1 in these birds, warning of potential wider spread and larger wildlife impacts. While the virus has not been detected on farms, biosecurity measures have been tightened to protect poultry. Human risk remains low, as there is no evidence of person-to-person transmission. The government has been preparing for the virus through enhanced biosecurity, shorebird testing, and response planning. The event was reported on August 3, 2026, with the mass mortality occurring on July 31, 2026. The minister emphasized the need for vigilance as the virus spreads through wildlife and the natural environment, potentially leading to significant losses. The situation is under investigation, and further details are expected.
Original source text (English)
AZ EN Region World AnewZ Originals AI & Next Business Culture Green Programmes Investigations Opinion Live Live Region World AnewZ Originals AI & Next Business Culture Green Programmes Investigations Opinion AnewZ Talks Follow Us Home Health Health news Bird Flu H5N1 bird flu kills dozens of seabirds as Australia warns of wider wildlife spread A test tube labelled "Bird Flu", eggs and a piece of paper in the colours of the Australian national flag are seen in this picture illustration, 14 January, 2023. reuters By Elnur Mirzazada , Reuters August 3, 2026 11:51 Copied Australia warned on Monday (3 August) of a wider spread of H5N1 bird flu after its first confirmed mass mortality event among seabirds killed about 50 greater crested terns off the coast of South Australia, south of Adelaide. Agriculture Minister Julie Collins said testing confirmed H5N1 in a group of 49 dead and 35 sick terns found during helicopter surveillance on rocks off Cape Jaffa, about 250 kilometres from Adelaide, on Friday. “Australians should expect to see more spread and larger numbers now of wildlife being affected,” Collins told reporters in Canberra. She said it was the first confirmed mass mortality event in Australia, warning that once H5 bird flu spreads through wildlife and the natural environment, significant losses cannot be avoided. Australia has recorded 74 confirmed cases across the country, from Western Australia to Queensland, although most have been in South Australia, Collins said. Farms remain unaffected There is no evidence so far of the virus on Australian farms, many of which have tightened biosecurity measures to protect poultry flocks since H5N1 was first detected in June. Australia and New Zealand, which confirmed its first H5N1 case last month, have spent years preparing for the virus through enhanced farm biosecurity, shorebird testing, vaccination of vulnerable species and response planning exercises. Human risk remains low The virus has killed hundreds of millions of birds and mammals in recent years, caused billions of U.S. dollars in losses for poultry farmers, infected cattle in the U.S. and left beaches littered with the bodies of birds and seals. H5N1 bird flu can infect humans who come into contact with infected animals, but global case numbers remain low and health officials say the risk to the public remains low. Time
The event was reported on August 3, 2026, with the mass mortality event occurring on Friday, July 31, 2026, and the warning issued on Monday, August 3, 2026.
Location and population
The event occurred off the coast of South Australia, specifically near Cape Jaffa, about 250 kilometers from Adelaide. It involved seabirds, particularly greater crested terns, and affected the broader Australian population.
Event
Australia confirmed its first mass mortality event of H5N1 bird flu among seabirds, with 50 greater crested terns killed and 35 sick terns found. Agriculture Minister Julie Collins stated that testing confirmed the presence of H5N1 in these birds.
Scale, impact and risk
The H5N1 bird flu has caused significant wildlife mortality, with 50 seabirds killed and 35 sick. The minister warned of potential wider spread and larger numbers of wildlife affected. Human risk remains low, as the virus has not been found on farms and does not spread between people.
Response, status and uncertainty
Australia has implemented enhanced biosecurity measures on farms and is conducting surveillance and testing. The minister emphasized preparedness and response planning, but the full extent of the spread and impact remains under investigation.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

银鸥威胁将 H5N1 病毒带入内陆,导致博丹岩 84 只燕鸥死亡 - 科技时报

Bird Flu Kills 84 Terns at Baudin Rock as Silver Gulls Threaten to Carry H5N1 Inland - Tech Times
新闻简报2026 年 8 月 1 日,澳大利亚在野生海鸟中确认了第一个 H5N1 进化枝 2.3.4.4b 暴发,此前空中机组人员在南澳大利亚附近的受保护小岛博丁岩统计出 84 只死亡大凤头燕鸥和 35 只重病的燕鸥。截至 8 月 2 日,全国五个州检测到 54 例 H5,其中仅南澳大利亚州就有 39 例。野生动物专家警告说,暴发 正在迅速升级,当地的鸟对鸟 传播 现已在多个沿海地点建立。银鸥是澳大利亚无处不在的食腐动物,被认为是可能将病毒携带到内陆和城市地区的关键 媒介。最令人担忧的是,海豹湾的濒临灭绝的澳大利亚海狮面临着受感染燕鸥尸体带来的迫在眉睫的 死亡率 风险,这与南美洲和亚南极赫德岛发生的灾难性 死亡率 事件相似,那里的 H5N1 杀死了 76% 的南象海豹幼崽。澳大利亚联邦科学与工业研究组织(CSIRO)正在进行验证性测试,而南澳大利亚州则暂停了海豹湾的海滩之旅,以限制人为干扰。
查看新闻五要素
时间
活动日期:2026年8月1日(航拍监测);发布日期:2026 年 8 月 2 日(科技时报)。时间范围:8 月 1 日之前的 24 小时期间,并于澳大利亚东部时间 8 月 2 日上午 9 点确认。
地点与对象
Baudin Rock(受保护的小岛,南澳大利亚贾法角以南约 5 公里);袋鼠岛海豹湾;海滩港、雅法角、绍森德、麦克唐奈港、罗布、尼尔森湾、波特兰(维多利亚);罗布(南澳大利亚)。受影响的物种:大凤头燕鸥、银鸥、澳大利亚海狮(濒临灭绝)、南象海豹(亚南极赫德岛)。
事件
澳大利亚首次在野生海鸟中确认 H5N1 进化枝 2.3.4.4b 暴发,对哺乳动物具有快速的局部 传播 和 跨物种溢出 风险。《科技时报》援引联邦 H5 禽流感仪表板、CSIRO、DAFF 监测页面、南澳大利亚州首席兽医官、伯内特研究所、澳大利亚鸟类保护协会、ABC 和 AAP 进行报道。状态:已确认 H5 检测;正在调查 H5N1 进化枝确认和哺乳动物 跨物种溢出。
规模、影响与风险
博丹岩 (Baudin Rock) 84 只大凤头燕鸥死亡(49 只确认死亡,35 只重病);截至澳大利亚东部标准时间 8 月 2 日上午 9 点,澳大利亚五个州的野生海鸟中检测到 54 例 H5(南澳大利亚州 39 例,维多利亚州新增 6 例);过去 24 小时内,南澳大利亚多个地点有 19 只大冠燕鸥确诊呈阳性。
应对、状态与不确定性
H5N1 进化枝 2.3.4.4b 等待 CSIRO 实验室确认;国家决定在野生动物中根除 H5N1 在技术上不再可行;南澳大利亚暂停海豹湾的海滩旅游,以减少人为干扰;监测地理扩散和生态路径的当局;野生动物兽医警告海狮即将来临 跨物种溢出;CSIRO 的澳大利亚疾病预防中心正在进行验证性测试。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bird Flu Kills 84 Terns at Baudin Rock as Silver Gulls Threaten to Carry H5N1 Inland - Tech Times

News Brief
Australia confirmed its first H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b outbreak in wild seabirds on August 1, 2026, after aerial crews counted 84 dead greater crested terns and 35 acutely ill at Baudin Rock, a protected islet off South Australia. The national tally reached 54 H5 detections across five states by August 2, with 39 in South Australia alone. Wildlife experts warn the outbreak is escalating rapidly, with local bird-to-bird transmission now established across multiple coastal sites. The silver gull, Australia’s ubiquitous scavenger, is identified as a key vector that could carry the virus inland and into urban areas. Of greatest concern, endangered Australian sea lions at Seal Bay face imminent spillover risk from infected tern carcasses, mirroring catastrophic mortality events in South America and on sub-Antarctic Heard Island, where H5N1 killed 76% of southern elephant seal pups. CSIRO is conducting confirmatory tests, while South Australia has suspended beach tours at Seal Bay to limit human disturbance.
Original source text (English)
Aerial surveillance crews scanning a remote South Australian islet on Friday, August 1, counted 49 dead greater crested terns and 35 more showing acute signs of illness — the largest single-site bird flu casualty count Australia has ever recorded, according to the federal H5 bird flu dashboard . In the same news cycle, a silver gull found at Robe became the first of its species anywhere in Australia to test positive for H5N1 , a development wildlife experts immediately identified as something qualitatively different from anything that had come before. South Australia's Chief Veterinary Officer Skye Fruean said it was "very unlikely" the terns at Baudin Rock — a protected islet about 5 kilometres (3.1 miles) south of Cape Jaffa — would test negative for H5. Laboratory confirmation from CSIRO's Australian Centre for Disease Preparedness was still pending at the time of reporting, but Fruean described the scene in terms that left little ambiguity: "We've known that this is going to happen at some point in time. Unfortunately, it does appear to be escalating quite rapidly ." Australia Just Crossed a Threshold It Cannot Walk Back From As of 9am Australian Eastern Standard Time on August 2, Australia had confirmed 54 H5 detections in wild seabirds across five states, according to the DAFF bird flu monitoring page . That count rose from 53 just hours earlier when six new cases were confirmed in Victoria — four in Portland and two in Nelson Bay — marking the first confirmed detections in that state. South Australia accounts for 39 of the national total, making it by far the outbreak's epicenter. The accelerating case count is not the most significant development. It is the pattern the cases reveal. The 19 greater crested terns confirmed positive over the 24 hours prior to the Baudin Rock discovery were collected from scattered points across the Limestone Coast and Kangaroo Island, including Beachport, Cape Jaffa, Southend, Port MacDonnell, Robe, and Seal Bay, as detailed in South Australia's H5 bird flu testing updates . That geographic dispersal, across multiple sites rather than a single point of entry, is the signature of established local, bird-to-bird transmission — not a new incursion from a migratory arrival. Australia's authorities confirmed this explicitly. The national decision that H5N1 is "no longer technically feasible to eradicate in wildlife" was made before this week's events. Why the Silver Gull Changes Everything The greater crested tern is a seabird. It nests on offshore islands, forages in coastal and open ocean waters, and moves primarily along the shoreline. When terns die in large numbers on a protected islet, the tragedy is real but geographically bounded. The virus stays near the water it arrived from. The silver gull ( Chroicocephalus novaehollandiae ) does not stay near the water. Australia's most familiar seabird is found on every beach, coastal suburb, rubbish tip, inland wetland, and freshwater reservoir in the country. It ranges from the shoreline into agricultural zones and urban centers. More critically for the current outbreak, it is a scavenger — a bird that eats what other birds leave behind, including carcasses . Sean Dooley, national affairs adviser at BirdLife Australia, identified the implication precisely. "There is such a large population of silver gulls, and they move into other habitats. They could be the vector that takes bird flu inland ," Dooley said. He noted that silver gulls "do come inland and can turn up in Lake Eyre following floods" — pointing to a geographic reach that dwarfs anything the tern population alone could achieve. Suman Majumdar, chief health officer at the Burnet Institute, was more direct about the mechanism: "Gulls move between beaches, wetlands, farmland and urban areas, which could carry the virus into a much wider range of wildlife, poultry and environments," Majumdar told AAP . What makes this especially concerning is not only the gull's range but the ecological pathway through which it acquires the virus. When infected terns die on beaches and islands, silver gulls scavenge the carcasses. Research from the South American outbreak documented exactly this process — where, as scientists described, "gull scavenging of pinniped carcasses and nesting on beaches amidst hundreds of tern carcasses" created sustained transmission cycles between species that would not otherwise have had direct contact. The silver gull detection in Australia signals that this same relay mechanism has now begun. Seal Bay: Where the Math Gets Frightening Of the 19 terns confirmed positive in the same 24-hour window as the Baudin Rock mass mortality event, five were found at Seal Bay on Kangaroo Island . Seal Bay is not merely another stretch of South Australian coastline. It is one of the most critical breeding grounds for the Australian sea lion ( Neophoca cinerea ) , a species listed as endangered under both Australian federal law and the IUCN Red List, with an estimated total global population of approximately 14,730 individuals. Wayne Boardman, a wildlife veterinarian at the University of Adelaide, did not soften his assessment. "The issue now with bird flu being found at Seal Bay is that it's almost certainly going to go from the birds into the sea lions," Boardman told the ABC . The virus spreads from terns to sea lions through a mechanism that colonial-breeding animals cannot easily escape. Sea lion pups and juveniles haul out in dense aggregations on beaches, making physical contact constant. Respiratory aerosols and contaminated substrate from infected terns dying nearby create direct exposure pathways. The structural biology of H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b compounds the danger: unlike low-pathogenicity flu strains that remain localized to the respiratory tract, HPAI H5N1 carries a multi-basic cleavage site in its hemagglutinin protein that allows it to be activated by enzymes present in virtually every organ, enabling systemic, rapidly lethal infection across mammalian hosts. The South American outbreak offers the nearest precedent. H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b killed an estimated 30,000 South American sea lions within six months during the 2022–23 outbreak across the continent's Pacific coast, according to research documenting the Argentina outbreak . On Australia's own sub-Antarctic Heard Island, between August 2025 and January 2026, the same clade killed approximately 13,359 of an estimated 17,364 southern elephant seal pups — a mortality rate averaging 76 percent across the island, and reaching 97 percent in the most severely affected harem, as documented in the McInnes et al. 2026 bioRxiv preprint . CSIRO laboratory confirmation identified H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b as the cause. Australian sea lions are not merely endangered — they are among the most slowly recovering large mammals on the continent. Unlike other pinnipeds that breed on annual cycles, Australian sea lions have an unusual aseasonal breeding cycle of 17 to 18 months, with each colony operating on its own schedule out of phase with others. This means a catastrophic pup mortality event cannot be buffered by a synchronized reproductive surge the following season. Population recovery, if it occurs at all, takes decades. The Seal Bay colony was already under pressure from historical sealing, fisheries bycatch, and entanglement before H5N1 arrived on its beach. South Australia's government suspended beach tours at Seal Bay earlier in the week to reduce human disturbance and minimize any risk of visitors inadvertently spreading virus between areas. The measure reflects a conservation logic that the Heard Island precedent now makes urgent: any additional stressor on a colony already in the path of an established outbreak accelerates the worst-case outcome. An Inland Signal at Monarto One confirmed case stands apart from the coastal pattern. A greater crested tern was found at Monarto — well away from the shoreline — in an area Fruean described as home to "production birds as well, as well as our zoo," meaning commercial poultry operations, according to the DAFF bird flu monitoring page . No infection has been confirmed in any commercial flock as of August 2. Australian Chief Veterinary Officer Beth Cookson reiterated that the risk to agriculture and human health remained low. The Monarto detection does not prove inland spread is established. But it demonstrates that infected seabirds are not confining themselves to the coastline, and that the geographic buffer between the current wildlife outbreak and the Australian poultry industry is narrower than the coastal-detection pattern alone would suggest. How Australia Got Here — and Why No One Can Stop What Happens Next H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b — the specific phylogenetic variant behind this global outbreak — emerged around 2020 from reassortment of earlier H5 viruses in East and Southeast Asia. By 2021 it had reached Europe and North America via migratory waterbird routes. By 2022 it was in South America. By 2023 it had reached Antarctica and the sub-Antarctic. Australia was the last inhabited continent free of the strain, protected partly by geography — migratory geese, which carry H5N1 efficiently over vast distances, do not use the same flyways as birds reaching Australian shores. What arrived in Australia in June 2026, through migratory seabirds returning from the Southern Ocean, was a virus with no prior exposure history in any Australian wildlife species. There is no acquired immunity in terns, gulls, sea lions, or any other resident species. Scientists cannot yet predict which Australian birds will prove most vulnerable, though experts see no reason to expect they would fare better than seabird populations devastated in Europe, the Americas, and the sub-Antarctic. Kate Millar, chief executive of BirdLife Australia, described the week's events as "a very significant escalation of the bird flu situation in Australia, and a very bleak milestone," adding that the country was now "at the beginning of a long and challenging battle," as quoted by IBTimes Australia . Jack Gough, chief executive of the Invasive Species Council, drew the South American parallel directly. "Starting to take off and mirroring the experience in South America of early detections, then rapid increases in numbers and spread leading to mass mortality events in wildlife," Gough said, framing the Baudin Rock discovery as not a surprise but a trajectory, according to AAP coverage of the event . Majumdar summarized what the current evidence implies: "What we are seeing now is the early part of that curve," as quoted by IBTimes Australia . What Australian Authorities Are Doing The federal Department of Agriculture's H5 dashboard is being updated daily. Ongoing aerial surveillance, Fruean said, was at least successfully tracking the geographic progression of the virus in real time. The national decision that eradication is not feasible has redirected the response from containment to mitigation. Under the National Management Agreement for H5 bird flu , state and territory governments are responsible for on-ground surveillance, industry liaison, and species-specific emergency responses. The Consultative Committee for Emergency Animal Disease has endorsed a framework for poultry biosecurity measures that took effect July 31 — a precautionary measure against the scenario the Monarto tern represents, in which an infected bird reaches the wrong environment at the wrong time, as confirmed in the DAFF bird flu updates . Federal Agriculture Minister Julie Collins told reporters that no evidence of the virus had been found in any agricultural or poultry system, and that human health risk remained low, as reported by Xinhua wire . For Australians who spend time near the coast or handle birds, the practical guidance from the Australian Centre for Disease Control is: do not touch sick or dead birds; report multiple dead birds to the 24-hour Emergency Animal Disease Hotline at 1800 675 888; and keep backyard poultry away from wild birds and their water sources. Frequently Asked Questions Is bird flu dangerous to humans in Australia right now? The risk to people in Australia is currently low. H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b does not spread easily to humans, and there has been no documented person-to-person transmission of this strain anywhere in the world. Australia's Centre for Disease Control advises that human infections generally follow close, direct contact with sick or dead birds. The guidance is simple and effective: do not touch dead or ill birds, keep children and pets away from carcasses on beaches, and report multiple dead birds by calling 1800 675 888. Why is the silver gull detection more alarming than the tern die-off? Greater crested terns are primarily coastal and pelagic birds — they live near the water and don't often range inland. Silver gulls are one of Australia's most geographically widespread species, moving regularly between coastlines, inland wetlands, freshwater systems, agricultural land, and urban areas. They are also scavengers, which means they may acquire H5N1 by feeding on infected tern carcasses. Once infected, a silver gull can carry the virus well beyond the coastal zones where the outbreak has so far been tracked. This is the pathway through which bird flu outbreaks in other countries reached poultry operations that were initially considered safely distant from wild bird activity. What does the Seal Bay situation mean for the Australian sea lion? The endangered Australian sea lion has a global population estimated at around 14,730 individuals, and the Seal Bay colony on Kangaroo Island represents one of the species' most significant breeding populations. Five infected terns have already been confirmed at that site, and University of Adelaide wildlife veterinarian Wayne Boardman said it was "almost certainly going to go from the birds into the sea lions." The concern is compounded by the species' unusual 17-to-18-month aseasonal breeding cycle, which means any mass pup mortality cannot be buffered by a synchronized reproductive recovery the following season. H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b killed approximately 13,359 southern elephant seal pups at Australia's own Heard Island between 2025 and 2026, and an estimated 30,000 South American sea lions during the 2022–23 Southern Hemisphere outbreak. What can I do to help protect Australian wildlife from bird flu? The most important actions for members of the public are: do not touch or pick up dead or ill seabirds or other wildlife; keep dogs and cats away from dead birds on beaches; report multiple dead birds or any dead marine mammal to the Emergency Animal Disease Hotline at 1800 675 888 or at birdflu.gov.au. Backyard poultry keepers should minimize wild bird access to feed, water, and housing — detailed guidance is available through state agriculture departments. Advocacy organizations including the Biodiversity Council and the Marine Conservation Society are calling for expanded federal investment in at-risk species monitoring and response capacity

Australia's first confirmed H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b outbreak in wild seabirds, with rapid local transmission and spillover risk to mammals. Reported by Tech Times citing federal H5 bird flu dashboard, CSIRO, DAFF monitoring page, South Australia's Chief Veterinary Officer, Burnet Institute, BirdLife Australia, ABC, and AAP. 警告信号已经结束:澳大利亚现在面临野生动物中毁灭性的禽流感 暴发 - 对话

The warning signs are over: Australia now faces a devastating bird flu outbreak in wildlife - The Conversation
新闻简报澳大利亚野生动物中的 H5N1 禽流感 暴发 疫情正在恶化,五个州已确认检测到 53 例,其中南澳大利亚州有 39 例涉及银鸥和大凤头燕鸥。监测 空中观测已发现雅法角附近海域有 49 只死亡燕鸥和 35 只患病燕鸥,正在等待确认。海豹湾是濒临灭绝的澳大利亚海狮的重要繁殖地,确认有五只受感染的燕鸥,引发了人们对潜在大规模 死亡率 事件的担忧,该事件类似于海外 死亡率 事件,当时 H5N1 导致数百万只鸟类和海豹死亡。虽然澳大利亚家禽或牲畜中尚未发现检测结果,但随着 野生鸟类 感染的增加,家禽面临的风险也在增加。当局敦促加强海洋哺乳动物的跨物种溢出,并对后院家禽采取严格的生物安全措施,以预防跨物种溢出。由于未发现病例,暴发 的真实规模仍不确定,长期传播预测也不可靠。

时间
发布时间:2026 年 8 月 1 日下午 6:38(美国东部时间);截至发布日期,活动仍在进行中。
地点与对象
澳大利亚(五个州),已在南澳大利亚州(罗布、石灰石海岸、袋鼠岛海豹湾、贾法角近海)确认检测到,并可能向内陆传播至南澳州莫纳托。受影响的野生动物包括野生海鸟(银鸥、大凤头燕鸥)、海豹、海狮和濒临灭绝的澳大利亚海狮。
事件
澳大利亚野生动物中 H5N1 死亡率 的 暴发 情况恶化,已确认当地的 传播 存在于常驻物种中,并存在潜在的大规模 死亡率 事件。《The Conversation》作为一项基于研究的分析进行了报道。
规模、影响与风险
五个州确认检测到 53 起野生海鸟;南澳大利亚州确诊39例,其中包括19只大凤头燕鸥和1只罗布银鸥。南澳州的空中 监测 发现了 49 只死亡和 35 只患病的大凤头燕鸥(等待确认)。海豹湾确认了五只受感染的燕鸥,这里是濒临灭绝的澳大利亚海狮的重要繁殖地。海外先例显示海豹和海狮的 死亡率 较高(例如,2023 年阿根廷有 17,000 只南象海豹,其中 95% 是幼崽 死亡率;赫德岛有约 13,000 只幼崽)。该菌株已导致多个大陆数百万人死亡。
应对、状态与不确定性
联邦和州政府制定了应对计划,增加了 监测,并为家禽业做好了 暴发 之前的准备。需要海豹和海狮的紧急监测。散养家禽的预防措施包括避免接触 野生鸟类、覆盖饲料和水、避免使用未经处理的地表水以及清洁鞋类和设备。家禽突然患病或死亡应立即报告。由于未发现病例,感染的真实数量仍然存在不确定性,并且无法可靠预测长期传播和对野生动物的影响。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

The warning signs are over: Australia now faces a devastating bird flu outbreak in wildlife - The Conversation

News Brief
Australia is experiencing a worsening H5N1 avian influenza outbreak in wildlife, with 53 confirmed detections across five states, including 39 cases in South Australia involving silver gulls and greater crested terns. Aerial surveillance has identified 49 dead and 35 sick terns offshore near Cape Jaffa, awaiting confirmation. Five infected terns were confirmed at Seal Bay, a critical breeding site for the endangered Australian sea lion, raising concerns about potential mass mortality events similar to overseas outbreaks where H5N1 killed millions of birds and seals. While no detections have been reported in Australian poultry or livestock, the risk to domestic birds is increasing as wild bird infections rise. Authorities urge enhanced surveillance of marine mammals and strict biosecurity measures for backyard poultry to prevent spillover. The true scale of the outbreak remains uncertain due to undetected cases, and long-term spread predictions are unreliable.

Home Arts + Culture Economy Education Environment + Energy Ethics + Religion Health Politics + Society Science + Tech World Podcasts Local En Español Edition Africa Australia Brasil Canada Canada (français) Català España Europe France Global Indonesia New Zealand United Kingdom United States Donate Skip to content Edition: United States Africa Australia Brasil Canada Canada (français) Català España Europe France Global Indonesia New Zealand United Kingdom s Donate Newsletters Become an author Sign up as a reader Sign in Academic rigor, journalistic flair Arts + Culture Economy Education Environment + Energy Ethics + Religion Health Politics + Society Science + Tech World Podcasts Local Thomas Parker/pexels The warning signs are over: Australia now faces a devastating bird flu outbreak in wildlife Published: August 1, 2026 6:38pm EDT The spread of H5N1 birdflu is worsening. At the time of writing, Australia has 53 confirmed detections in wild seabirds across five states. In South Australia there are 39 confirmed cases, including a silver gull at Robe and 19 greater crested terns found along the Limestone Coast and Seal Bay on Kangaroo Island. South Australian authorities are also investigating 49 dead and 35 sick greater crested terns found by aerial surveillance teams at an offshore location south of Cape Jaffa. Samples are still being tested, but if confirmed, this would represent the first mass mortality of Australian animals associated with the virus. Until recently, Australia’s detections were mostly isolated migratory seabirds arriving from the Southern Ocean. But these detections show this has changed, with local transmission among wildlife that live in Australia. Which species are most at risk? Animals that gather densely in breeding colonies face the greatest danger. Terns, gulls, gannets and albatrosses can transmit the virus rapidly through close contact and contaminated environments, with these groups experiencing devastating mortality in other parts of the world. The silver gull infections are particularly concerning because they move between beaches, towns, rubbish tips, wetlands and inland waterways. A suspected case at Monarto, SA, does not prove H5N1 is yet established inland, but it shows infected seabirds cannot be “coastally contained”. Seals and sea lions also require urgent surveillance. H5N1 killed more than 17,000 southern elephant seals in Argentina in 2023, with 95% pup mortality at one monitored colony. Around 13,000 elephant seal pups later died on Heard Island. While it was not possible to carry out postmortem examinations on all individuals at this remote location, H5N1 was confirmed as present and recorded as the most likely cause of death. Five infected terns have now been confirmed at Seal Bay, a critical breeding site for the endangered Australian sea lion. Australian sea lions and fur seals breed in dense colonies where disease could spread quickly. Australian sea lions on a beach in South Australia. Seals and sea lions require urgent surveillance - many thousands have died from H5N1 overseas. Phil Kirkpatrick/flickr, CC BY Can we predict how far it will spread? We cannot reliably predict the spread of H5N1 in Australia. The 53 confirmed detections are not the true number of infections – they are the birds found, reported and tested. Many infected animals will never be detected. Cases from the rest of the world show this strain of H5N1 is capable of spreading huge distances, carried by migratory wildlife. Every other continent, including Antarctica, has been infected by this strain of H5N1 over the past five years, with deaths in the millions. Hundreds of species of birds have been infected, and the virus has evolved to spread more efficiently between mammals. This pattern makes it very difficult to confidently predict how the virus will spread in Australia, but we must be prepared for a widespread and devastating outbreak. What about poultry farms and backyard chooks? There are still no detections of H5N1 in Australian poultry or other livestock, but the risk has increased as confirmed infections rise among resident wild birds. Wild birds can introduce influenza to poultry through direct contact or by contaminating water, feed, footwear and equipment. Backyard owners should prevent contact with wild birds, cover feed and water, avoid untreated surface water, and clean footwear and equipment. Sudden illness or deaths should be reported immediately. Australia’s response now needs to evolve Sadly, the impacts of bird flu on species and ecosystems will play out for decades to come. Australia’s pre-outbreak preparation and coordination has been strong. Federal and state governments established response plans, increased surveillance and prepared the poultry industry before H5N1 arrived. As wild bird infections increase, the poultry industry needs to be supported in implementing biosecurity procedures, and strategic surveillance is needed for both farms and wildlife. Reliable mortality estimates are essential to understand the long-term impacts, and support species to recover. Australia should also assess whether vaccination could protect vulnerable marine mammals before mass deaths begin. We cannot stop wild birds from flying. But we can decide whether this becomes an unmanaged wildlife catastrophe or a closely tracked outbreak, with farms and vulnerable species given timely protection. Please do not touch sick or dead birds or marine mammals. Record the location, take photographs from a safe distance, keep pets away, and report the sighting to the Emergency Animal Disease Hotline on 1800 675 888. Time
Published: August 1, 2026 6:38pm EDT; event ongoing as of publication date.
Location and population
Australia (five states), with confirmed detections in South Australia (Robe, Limestone Coast, Seal Bay on Kangaroo Island, Cape Jaffa offshore), and potential inland spread to Monarto, SA. Affected wildlife includes wild seabirds (silver gulls, greater crested terns), seals, sea lions, and endangered Australian sea lions.
Event
A worsening outbreak of H5N1 avian influenza in Australian wildlife, with confirmed local transmission among resident species and potential mass mortality events. Reported by The Conversation as a research-based analysis.
Scale, impact and risk
53 confirmed detections in wild seabirds across five states; 39 confirmed cases in South Australia, including 19 greater crested terns and a silver gull at Robe. Aerial surveillance in SA identified 49 dead and 35 sick greater crested terns (awaiting confirmation). Five infected terns confirmed at Seal Bay, a critical breeding site for endangered Australian sea lions. Overseas precedents show high mortality in seals and sea lions (e.g., 17,000 southern elephant seals in Argentina in 2023, 95% pup mortality; ~13,000 pups on Heard Island). The strain has caused deaths in millions across multiple continents.
Response, status and uncertainty
Federal and state governments established response plans, increased surveillance, and prepared the poultry industry pre-outbreak. Urgent surveillance of seals and sea lions is required. Prevention measures for backyard poultry include avoiding contact with wild birds, covering feed and water, avoiding untreated surface water, and cleaning footwear and equipment. Sudden illness or deaths in poultry should be reported immediately. Uncertainty remains regarding the true number of infections due to undetected cases, and the long-term spread and impact on wildlife cannot be reliably predicted.

Australia to Be Dealing Avian Influenza Outbreak for Long Period - teleSUR English
新闻简报在全国病例数超过 100 例后,澳大利亚正面临 H5N1 禽流感 暴发 的扩展,其中南澳大利亚州有 81 例,维多利亚州也发现了更多病例。联邦政府确认南澳大利亚海岸附近的大凤头燕鸥发生了该大陆首次大规模 死亡率 事件,但当局报告称农业系统中没有发生 死亡率 事件。农业部长朱莉·柯林斯 (Julie Collins) 和首席兽医官贝丝·库克森 (Beth Cookson) 强调了 暴发 可能持续时间长和传染性高,并指出,虽然预防措施到位,但该疾病的毒力使遏制变得复杂。据描述,对人类健康的风险较低,专家们正在就保护受威胁物种和农业部门提出建议。环境部长默里·瓦特 (Murray Watt) 预计,随着局势的发展,还会出现更多大规模 死亡率 事件。

Australia to Be Dealing Avian Influenza Outbreak for Long Period - teleSUR English

News Brief
Australia is confronting an extended H5N1 avian influenza outbreak in wild birds after the national case count surpassed 100, with 81 cases in South Australia and additional detections in Victoria. The federal government confirmed the continent’s first mass mortality event in greater crested terns off South Australia’s coast, while authorities report no spillover into agricultural systems. Agriculture Minister Julie Collins and Chief Veterinary Officer Beth Cookson emphasized the outbreak’s likely long duration and high contagion, noting that while preventive measures are in place, the disease’s virulence complicates containment. The risk to human health is described as low, and experts are advising on safeguarding threatened species and farm sectors. Environment Minister Murray Watt anticipates further mass mortality events as the situation evolves.
Original source text (English)
Australia to Be Dealing Avian Influenza Outbreak for Long Period X/ @1shareShare1 August 4, 2026 Hour: 7:21 am 🔗 Comparte este artículo PDF New detections of the H5N1 strain in South Australia and Victoria have taken the national number of cases to 102. On Tuesday, Agriculture Minister Julie Collins said that Australia could be dealing with the H5N1 strain of avian influenza for a long period of time as the number of confirmed cases surpassed 100. RELATED: Italy Confirms Europe’s First Human Case of H9N2 Avian Flu Authorities have increased surveillance of wild birds following local transmission of the deadly strain among greater crested terns and seagulls. The government is listening to experts on how to prevent the strain from spreading to threatened species and agricultural systems, but that the country could be dealing with the outbreak for a “long period of time.” “We have learnt from overseas experience. We’ve done everything we can, but we cannot stop a very virulent, very contagious disease in this bird flu,” Collins said. Australian Chief Veterinary Officer Beth Cookson said that new confirmed detections of the H5N1 strain in South Australia (SA) and Victoria have taken the national total number of cases to 102, 81 of which have been in SA. Cookson said that there have been no detections in Australia’s agricultural system and the risk to human health remains low. Speaking on ABC radio separately on Tuesday, Environment and Water Minister Murray Watt said that Australia is likely to see more mass mortality events. On Monday, the federal government confirmed the Australian continent’s first mass mortality event from the H5N1 strain in a flock of greater crested terns that were found on an island off the SA coast. teleSUR/ JF Source: Xinhua

Scale, impact and risk
102 confirmed cases nationwide (81 in South Australia); first mass mortality event in greater crested terns; no detections in agricultural systems; risk to human health remains low.
Response, status and uncertainty

在不起眼的海鸥身上检测到 H5N1 禽流感有可能将禽流感传播到海滩以外

H5N1 detected in humble seagull threatens to spread bird flu beyond the beach
新闻简报在南澳大利亚的银鸥中检测到该病毒后,澳大利亚进入了 H5N1 禽流感 传播的新阶段,范围扩大到之前受影响的迁徙海鸟和燕鸥之外。2.3.4.4b 分支菌株已在全球范围内造成大规模死亡,现在有可能通过经常出没海滩、城市、垃圾填埋场和湿地的高度流动的海鸥迁移到内陆。当局报告南澳大利亚有 49 只大凤头燕鸥死亡,并警告海鸥和燕鸥的聚集事件可能会成为超级传播事件。虽然尚未报告人间病例,但官员们正在考虑采取措施,例如对散养家禽的安置令,同时评估对家禽和国际重要湿地(如库隆湿地)的风险,库隆湿地是数百万候鸟的重要中转站。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-08-03;该事件被描述为发生在 2026 年 8 月上旬,时间范围为 2026 年 6 月中旬至 8 月上旬。
地点与对象
南澳大利亚州、维多利亚州以及澳大利亚全国;受影响的种群包括银鸥(海鸥)、大凤头燕鸥、迁徙海鸟(巨海燕、棕贼鸥)和可能的家禽(鸡)。
事件
在南澳大利亚的银鸥中检测到 H5N1 禽流感,标志着除了先前受影响的迁徙海鸟和燕鸥之外,本地传播进入了新阶段;美国广播公司新闻 (ABC News) 和澳大利亚首席兽医官贝丝·库克森 (Beth Cookson) 报道;事件已确认并正在进行中。
规模、影响与风险
南澳大利亚州发现 49 只大凤头燕鸥死亡;由于银鸥的流动性和与其他鸟类的聚集,有可能广泛传播;进一步传播至家禽(例如鸡)和淡水鸟类(例如鸭)的风险;H5N1 进化枝 2.3.4.4b 此前曾在全球范围内造成大规模死亡,包括海鸥和海豹。
应对、状态与不确定性
澳大利亚和南澳大利亚当局进行监测和调查;监测 正在进行中;关于散养家禽的强制住房令的讨论;所提供的证据中没有报告人类病例;病毒被认为留在这里;关于未来传播到水禽和内陆湿地的尚未解决的问题。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

H5N1 detected in humble seagull threatens to spread bird flu beyond the beach

News Brief
Australia has entered a new phase of H5N1 avian influenza spread after the virus was detected in silver gulls in South Australia, expanding beyond previously affected migratory seabirds and terns. The clade 2.3.4.4b strain, which has caused mass die-offs globally, now threatens to move inland via highly mobile gulls that frequent beaches, cities, landfills, and wetlands. Authorities report 49 dead greater crested terns in South Australia and warn that congregation events among gulls and terns could act as super-spreader events. While no human cases are reported, officials are weighing measures such as housing orders for free-range poultry as they assess risks to domestic birds and internationally significant wetlands like the Coorong, a critical stopover for millions of migratory shorebirds.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content Skip to news navigation, settings and search Explore the ABC More from ABC Close menuABC ABC News ABC iview ABC listen Kids & Students Indigenous Information & Services news iview listen Loading ABC News Just In For You Politics World Business Analysis Sport Lifestyle Entertainment Quizzes More Search News Home Just In For You Analysis Rural Watch Live Health Indigenous Politics Science Deep Time Elections World Environment Investigations Business Local news Quizzes Sport AFL NRL Football Tennis Cricket Netball Lifestyle Wellbeing Relationships & Family Food & Recipes Personal Finance Home & Garden Entertainment TV & Movies Books Music Pop Culture Arts ABC News News Home Science H5N1 detected in humble seagull threatens to spread bird flu beyond the beach ShareShare article analysis H5N1 detected in humble seagull threatens to spread bird flu beyond the beach By environment reporter Peter de Kruijff ABC Science Topic:Avian Influenza Posted Mon 3 Aug 2026 at 3:53pmMon 3 Aug 2026 at 3:53pm Mon 3 Aug 2026 at 3:53pm Silver gulls are highly resourceful birds that move between shorelines, cities and inland environments. (iNaturalist: Darcy Whittaker, Silver gull, CC BY-NC 4.0) It's hard not to think of Australia's hotel quarantine system during the COVID-19 pandemic while waiting to see what would happen with the animal equivalent, a global bird flu panzootic, on our shores. In the early stages of the COVID response we were given the daily numbers of returning Australians testing positive to the virus. All the while we held our breath waiting to see if it would spread out into the wider community. It's been a similar feeling as migratory birds from Antarctic waters started washing up in mid-June with the H5N1 avian influenza that has swept the globe killing millions of animals. Infected giant petrels and a brown skua, that travel seasonally to chill in the ocean off Australia in winter, were kept in vet clinics as we kept our fingers crossed there would be no local spread. But now the humble seagull getting H5N1, coupled with a mass mortality of greater crested terns, marks a new stage for bird flu's spread in Australia. Until the first seagull was detected with H5 in South Australia last week, the only birds with the virus were terns and those migratory seabirds which stick to the ocean or beaches. But seagulls, the common name for the silver gull species, can move vast distances around the country taking advantage of resources be it nicking chips or hanging out at tips. Potential for long-distance spread Seagulls can be found nearly everywhere in Australia, from beaches to cities, freshwater systems, mountains and landfills. They even pop up in the middle of the country when big rains fill up sites like Kati Thanda-Lake Eyre. This lifestyle gives them the potential to act as a crossover species in spreading the virus to a range of different environments and other bird species. "They're more cosmopolitan in their requirements, [eating at] tips, shopping alleys," Chris Purnell, the wetland and migratory shorebirds manager at Birdlife Australia, told me. "They'll mingle with everything and often parasitise [steal from] other birds and eat their eggs." Seagulls also poo in a lot of places, which is one of the big ways the virus spreads. They spend a lot of time around greater crested terns, which have tested positive to H5N1 in SA and Victoria so far, mixing together in large groups. Both species are coming into their nesting seasons where they can form colonies of thousands. Silver gulls in South Australia nest between June and December with the exact timing varying colony to colony. Silver gulls and greater crested terns often mingle on beaches around Australia. (iNaturalist: Tim, Great crested tern, CC BY 4.0) To borrow a term from the COVID-19 pandemic, those are potential super-spreader events. And they are a real concern when this particular type of H5N1, the clade 2.3.4.4b, wiped out millions of wild and domestic birds and thousands of seals around the world in just a few years, including gulls. The black-headed gull, a close relative of the silver gull, has been the subject of mass die-offs in Europe from this type of H5N1, with an estimated 51,000 adult birds dying in 2023 in Poland. Mass bird deaths are a live concern given what has happened elsewhere in the world and with the first Australian mass mortality of 49 dead greater crested terns over the weekend in SA. Australian Chief Veterinary Officer Beth Cookson said on Monday it had taken a little longer than expected to see a localised spread occurring since the first migratory bird detections. "Now that we have got that localised spread, this is following a fairly predictable curve in terms of the number of infections," she said. "Particularly during a period of year as we might start to see birds start to congregate more. "We know when birds and other animals congregate that's where we get more risk of circulation of the virus and onward spread." H5N1 here to stay Technical advice last week to the federal government puts Australia past the point where the virus may have been eradicated, particularly with a local spread occurring in SA. Skye Fruean, the chief veterinary officer of South Australia, said the disease was here to stay. "Once we started to get that really strong evidence of that transmission between the crested terns, that does change things in terms of our decision making." Loading Dr Fruean told SA Country Hour on Friday that surveillance was still the primary focus but state and federal governments were looking now at what else they should be doing in terms of virus management. That includes discussions around what should trigger a mandatory housing order for free-range chicken operations. "At this point in time it is an option open to jurisdictions," Dr Fruean said. Bringing chickens indoors keeps them away from the potential exposure to water contaminated by wild infected birds and the latter's faeces. That's because chickens are highly susceptible to this strain and more than 100 million have died or been culled in the US since its first outbreak in 2022. But it's still early days yet, with states weighing up the risks and whether chickens need to be locked down. Duck, duck, virus The biggest turning point for different states could be when the virus shows up in water fowl like wild ducks. Ducks are thought to have co-evolved with bird flu viruses like H5 as they can be silent spreaders showing little signs of illness. The virus also spreads rapidly among ducks as it can survive for multiple days within freshwater bodies that ducks both defecate in and eat from. It's not out of the realms of possibility that a bird such as a seagull or a pelican could now be the bridging species that takes the virus from the coast and brings it to ducks. When I rang Dr Purnell on Friday he was visiting Victoria's volcanic lake system, west of Melbourne, where seagulls were starting to gather for nesting. Ducks and other freshwater birds are also highly mobile and move between major water systems. So, the virus has the potential to move big distances and make its way around the country from a single incursion. Past tracking studies of species like ibis and spoonbill, for instance, on the east coast of Australia show a 2,000 kilometre flyway over the Murray Darling Basin. Individual birds were tracked flying all along the system and up to northern Queensland. With the local spread of the virus in SA, there are major areas of concern for initial outbreaks in internationally significant wetlands like the Coorong at the mouth of the Murray River. The Coorong is home to more than 200 bird species, and is a major stopover for millions of migratory shorebirds which start arriving in September from the northern hemisphere each year. They too will be vulnerable to the virus. While we can't predict what exactly will happen from now with the virus's spread, any wishful thinking it wouldn't establish in local birds is past. And as federal agriculture minister Julie Collins said on Monday, we should expect to see an increase in the number of birds getting H5N1 in Australia. "Sadly, we do know that once the H5 bird flu is spreading in wildlife and the natural environment, it is not possible to avoid significant losses, which is what we are starting to see now," she said. "Australians should expect to see more [H5] spread and larger numbers now of wildlife being affected." Anyone who sees a dead or sick bird is asked not to touch it and report it to the Emergency Animal Disease Hotline on 1800 675 888.
